Not a written source, but Crash Course is too useful for solving common knowledge gaps to not include\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t2. Watch Crash Course while you\'re in line, waiting for someone, on hold, brushing your teeth, etc. Any time you could be scrolling on your phone, watch Crash Course instead.\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t3. **Top Five LSAT Relevant Crash Course Sections**: Intellectual Property (Ellen\'s favorite for LSAT students), Economics, US Government & Politics, Art History, and Philosophy\\n\\n3\\n\\n### Vocab Training\\n\\n### Once A Week, Do This Instead\\n\\n1\\n\\n### Translation Marathon\\n\\n8 sections\\n\\n≥ 1x per week\\n\\n## Ellen\'s Advice\\n\\n### Why Are We Doing This?\\n\\nThe Translation + CLIR Drills in this Routine are the foundation of your entire LSAT prep journey. This is the most important homework you will do during your LSAT prep training, even compared to PTs.\\n\\nThis Routine builds a foundation in LR Translation before we port your Translation skills over to RC in the next Routine.\\n\\n### How This Should Be Done\\n\\nYour progression toward the Translation time and accuracy goals is primarily determined by two things: **volume** and **following the instructions.**\\n\\nTo maximize your progress in this Routine, do all your Translation + CLIR Drills consecutively; do not take breaks between Translation + CLIR Drills. Do not review your Translation + CLIR Drills in between sections. All review should be done in a separate sitting.\\n\\n**Do at least 3 consecutive Translation + CLIR Drills per day, if possible.** Ideally, you would complete 6 Translation + CLIR drills consecutively per day. Doing 1 Translation + CLIR Drill per day will not improve your skills; it will keep your skills the same. You will only start improving on your second consecutive Translation + CLIR Drill; this is why it is so useful to do *at least* 3 consecutive Translation + CLIR Drills every day.\\n\\nAdd a Translation Marathon to your Daily Practice at least once per week. If you can manage to do Translation + CLIR Marathons more often, you will progress to the time goal faster.\\n\\nExtracurricular Reading and Vocab should always be done at the end of your Routine to gradually expand your common sense in the subject areas where you\'re weakest. If you struggle with science passages, emphasize daily science engagement in the Extracurricular Reading. If you struggle with abstract language, emphasize daily humanities engagement in the Extracurricular Reading.\\n\\n### How to Move On\\n\\n**Once you are halfway to your Translation + CLIR Drill time goal, move to the next Routine: Translation + Decaf.**\\n\\n**The time goal for Translation + CLIR Drills is 20 minutes.** The accommodated time goals for Translation + CLIR Drills are 25 minutes for 53-minute accommodations and 30 minutes for 70-minute accommodations. Meeting this Translation + CLIR Drill time goal is only valid under two conditions: you have a CLIR for every stimulus and you have translated and quipped every stimulus in the section out loud. Ideally, you should hit -5 accuracy against the CLIR Answer Key consistently in order to beat the time goal, but this accuracy goal may be adjusted according to your score goals.\\n\\n**Example of When to Move On**: You started at 52 minutes per out loud Translation + CLIR Drill section and you don\'t have accommodations, so your time goal is 20 minutes. The midpoint of 52 and 20 is 36, so you would move on when your time hits 36 minutes.\\r\\nFILE: How to Camo • LO.md\\r\\n---\\ntitle: \\"How to Camo • LO\\"\\nsource: \\"https://new.theloophole.com/training-camp/browse/stage/accuracy/video/how-to-camo\\"\\nauthor:\\npublished:\\ncreated: 2026-08-30\\ndescription: \\"LSAT prep from Ellen Cassidy, author of The Loophole in LSAT Logical Reasoning — video lessons, drills, and guided study plans.\\"\\ntags:\\n  - \\"clippings\\"\\n---\\n## How to Camo\\n\\n0:14\\n\\n/\\n\\n8:25\\n\\n- 0:00How to Camo so I comment on Camo in other videos But I just want to have one dedicated video in case anyone\'s bopping around and they were just interested to learn about Camo solo independent of any other topic\\n- 0:11and so Camo what is it? it is a way to review your sections in which you put in all the answers that you chose in a section and Camo will generate a list of questions for you to return to that includes all of your wrong answers and an unknown number of decoy correct answers\\n- 0:26and so You then have this anonymized list you go back through all of the questions you redo them earnestly And then you place your new answers in Camo you tell it what you pick this time And then it sorts your mistakes into conceptual gaps those are the questions you missed twice you missed both\\n- 0:40in the original take of the Section and in your Camo pass miss reads which are the questions you missed in your original take and you got right in your Camo pass Self-doubt\\n- 0:48which are questions you got right and were decoys and then they they come back wrong in the Camo Changed away from right answer and self-confidence Those are the ones that you got right both times a decoy that comes up that actually you stay with your answer Which is great\\n- 1:02So though that is how you Camo it gives you a lot of really great information because of course Miss reads questions that you were able to correct those are really low-hanging fruit that we want to focus on in terms of like okay What exactly did I do in the moment? Just when I was doing this section\\n- 1:15originally that I can now change that Enabled me to get it right in camera you have a lot more clues to go on because you actually did get it right in Camo so like what did you do differently?\\n- 1:24And then you have your conceptual gaps These are the ones that you got wrong in Camo again either by choosing the same answer or choosing a different answer Those are our more sticky ones and ones that we\'ll need to review more fully Maybe put on The Island to come back to later But they also\\n- 1:38they give us a different sort of clue than the miss reads of okay These are the ones that I genuinely there\'s like something missing that I don\'t understand and we need to resolve it over time\\n- 1:46A self-doubt that\'s where you know you got it right and changed wrong these indicate an issue With second-guessing yourself obviously self-doubt that\'s the name of it\\n- 1:55And it means that you need to work on using your gut instinct and not questioning yourself going with your gut and not Staying around to undo your good thoughts\\n- 2:05and so those are the basics of just what Camo is and how you go about it now Let me give you the kind of finer points to enable you to Camo really really well because\\n- 2:12there are a few things that people Do with Camo that really mess them up And so first thing is you should wait at least 24 hours After doing a section and before Camoing it\\n- 2:23and why do I say this I say this because when people Camo right after they do A section their score is always worse\\n- 2:29Because they are essentially using Camo as a way to check the section and all they really want is the score\\n- 2:34They don\'t really care as much about actually doing well on the Camo they tend to rush it They tend to just choose the same answers that they chose originally under some kind of weird guise of like My integrity says that I chose this\\n- 2:46and I don\'t want to change you know and cheat or something That that weird thing that people do in camera sometimes It\'s not exclusive to doing the section right after you did it originally,\\n- 2:56but you\'re allowed to change your answer in Camo It\'s not like dishonest to have a new idea And so don\'t just stick with your same answers out of some weird misguided sense of loyalty.\\n- 3:04That\'s not like a real thing And so you should wait at least 24 hours Simply because it will improve your Camo score because you\'re more likely to genuinely redo the questions\\n- 3:14You\'ll have a much more level head when you go about them And of course instead of waiting 24 hours You could also have a Camo day once or twice a week where you extend out greater than 24 hours before you Camo which the The reason for it just kind of compounds of like the longer you are away from\\n- 3:30it the more level-headed the less emotional and the more Insights you will be able to glean from your sections, especially doing them in a bigger batch\\n- 3:37Another Camo pro tip do not do Camo Before you prep for the day before you do all your sections do your Camo as the last thing you do in your prep when I build routines for people of what they\'re gonna do every day\\n- 3:48I always put Camo at the end and they always move it to first because they just want to check their sections and get the scores\\n- 3:53But the reason why I always put it at the end is because I don\'t want you being Reactive to your Camo results and immediately being like oh, I just have a problem with Method\\n- 4:02Okay, now I need to make sure I get all the Methods right which like will literally do nothing to actually improve your Method performance But like sure\\n- 4:08Just stay patient remember the results exist without you knowing the results like you knowing the results and the results existing are not the same thing\\n- 4:16and so When when you\'ve waited to Camo wonderful now make sure that you have not actually checked the section\\n- 4:24Before you Camo so the this is a bit tricky in law hub because with law hub when you press complete section or you do The test in exam mode it will auto check the section for you And it literally reveals the right answers to every question and the score and it\'s really really freaking annoying\\n- 4:39In order to get around this problem what I would like you to do is just open the section and open all the sections You\'re gonna do in different tabs on your browser And then after you\'ve chosen your answers write them down separately so you have a record and then just close the tab It\'s not as though like you know goes away or anything But then you are not automatically put into that complete section bucket You don\'t have to press complete section and always do your tests in exam, so we don\'t want that\\n- 5:12So you also when you go back through the sections on law hub sometimes if you are you\'re just browsing around you can accidentally mouse Over the section on like the overall menu area where it\'s like listing all the pts and it\'ll tell you the score there as well So be aware there too\\n- 5:26just because you don\'t want to know the score before you Camo 5:59when we go through we want to do the question from start to finish We still want to translate and CLIR that stimulus.\\n- 6:04We still want to go through the question stem We still want to read and translate all the answers and do our full process\\n- 6:09if we\'re not totally sure of our answer We we could hyper skip it in the Camo and come back We would do our normal question remediation processes that we would do in a real section because we want to get that question Right in Camo.\\n- 6:19That\'s the whole point Very often I will find people only go back to their final two answers Especially if they\'re doing the section right after they initially completed it\\n- 6:27They won\'t actually redo the question or like I said that misguided sense of loyalty thing where it\'s like well I chose this and I don\'t really know that it\'s wrong. So\\n- 6:35I stay with it or whatever This also kind of goes in line with just trying to rush through the questions and go super fast Which we also don\'t want to do\\n- 6:46you can say to yourself, okay if I get seven questions back in Camo They aren\'t all right,\\n- 6:51you know, some of them are gonna be wrong So it would be a bad idea to stay with all of the answers that you chose\\n- 6:56Like game the camel a little bit, you know If you\'ve got seven questions back you\'ve got to change some of them, right? Because\\n- 7:02our goal is to get a minus zero on this Camo and so we want to move toward that with with vigor\\n- 7:07And the last thing is do your Wrong Answer Journal while you\'re Camoing So after you\'ve you know put in all of your Camo stuff and it tells you your conceptual gaps tells you your misreads all that That\'s the perfect time to go Wrong Answer Journaling\\n- 7:19And so at that point it\'s a wonderful time start filling that in right then when the question is fresh\\n- 7:23instead of putting it off You really want a Wrong Answer Journal as you go rather than Wrong Answer Journaling in one big bash\\n- 7:30if you start And by as you go I mean as you Camo rather than putting off a Wrong Answer Journaling into a separate sitting because if you allow Wrong Answer Journaling to pile up as like a Big task what happens is it just doesn\'t happen and then you Wrong Answer Journal nothing\\n- 7:43If you blow off Wrong Answer Journaling for a time and then you start to build up a debt honestly, just write off those past sections don\'t Wrong Answer Journal them and try and start fresh of Okay, the ones I do today though I\'m gonna Wrong Answer Journal those because very often the debt\\n- 7:57of having so many in the past a Wrong Answer Journal from the far Past will keep people from doing it at all because they know they could never get out of debt And it\'s also true that your sections from like three weeks ago That you got wrong and stuff like that those wrong answers are not\\n- 8:11as valuable to Wrong Answer Journal at this stage You would get a lot more return on your investment from Wrong Answer Journaling things that happened much much more recently like things to Camo today\\n- 8:20And so I hope that that helps. I hope that tells you how to Camo and I can\'t wait to see how you do\\n\\n##### In This Training Block\\n\\n### Ellen\'s Notes\\n\\n***Camo Tips***\\n\\nNever check the answer key on any section before Camoing\\n\\nWait 24 hours before Camoing\\n\\nNever press Complete Section in LawHub\\n\\nDo your Wrong Answer Journal when you Camo\\n\\n### Take Notes\\n\\n## Video Course Review\\n\\n[\\n\\nConcept Review\\n\\n](https://new.theloophole.com/training-camp/concept-review)\\r\\nFILE: How and Why to Do Decaf RC Translation • LO.md\\r\\n---\\ntitle: \\"How and Why to Do Decaf RC Translation • LO\\"\\nsource: \\"https://new.theloophole.com/training-camp/browse/concept-review/rc-translation-strategy/how-and-why-to-do-decaf-rc-translation\\"\\nauthor:\\npublished:\\ncreated: 2026-08-30\\ndescription: \\"LSAT prep from Ellen Cassidy, author of The Loophole in LSAT Logical Reasoning — video lessons, drills, and guided study plans.\\"\\ntags:\\n  - \\"clippings\\"\\n---\\n### Ellen\'s Notes\\n\\n***Decaf RC Translation Drill***\\n\\n**NOTE**: If you want to do your recaps out loud in the beginning to keep yourself honest about the quality and completeness of the recaps, that\'s totally fine. Transition to silent recaps when you feel like you\'re ready.\\n\\n1. Read, translate, and quip paragraph 1. Do it just like LR.\\n\\t1. This means all the details are included just like they would be on LR.\\n\\t\\t2. Quip after *every* Translation.\\n\\t\\t3. This standard applies to every paragraph you ever translate on RC.\\n2. Look away from paragraph 1 and recap the **FULL TRANSLATION** of paragraph 1 silently.\\n\\t1. The recap is not a summary, gist, or main point.\\n\\t\\t2. The recap should not omit names, dates, and lists.\\n\\t\\t3. The recap should not be a shopping list of information. It should be a story in your own words.\\n\\t\\t4. It is encouraged to mix up the order of the sentences and ideas.\\n\\t\\t5. This standard applies to every paragraph (or collection of paragraphs) you ever recap on RC.\\n3. Read, translate, and quip paragraph 2. Do it just like LR.\\n4. Look away from paragraph 2 and recap the **FULL TRANSLATION** of paragraph 2 silently.\\n5. Without looking back, recap the Translations of paragraphs 1 + 2 back to back silently.\\n6. Read, translate, and quip paragraph 3. Do it just like LR.\\n7. Look away from paragraph 3 and recap the **FULL TRANSLATION** of paragraph 3 silently.\\n8. Without looking back, recap the Translations of paragraphs 1 + 2 + 3 back to back silently.\\n9. Repeat this pattern for all remaining paragraphs.\\r\\nFILE: How and Why to Use the Island • LO.md\\r\\n---\\ntitle: \\"How and Why to Use the Island • LO\\"\\nsource: \\"https://new.theloophole.com/training-camp/browse/concept-review/how-to-raise-your-score/how-and-why-to-use-the-island\\"\\nauthor:\\npublished:\\ncreated: 2026-08-30\\ndescription: \\"LSAT prep from Ellen Cassidy, author of The Loophole in LSAT Logical Reasoning — video lessons, drills, and guided study plans.\\"\\ntags:\\n  - \\"clippings\\"\\n---\\n### Ellen\'s Notes\\n\\n***The Island***\\n\\n**NOTE**: The Island is a spreadsheet you make yourself with one column called Island and another column called Canoed Away.\\n\\n1. If a question is still mystifying after Camo, just add the citation of the question to the Island\\n\\t1. This means at least one of these three things is true:\\n\\t\\t1. I don\'t understand why the right answer is right.\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t2. I don\'t understand why the wrong answer is wrong.\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t3. I have no idea how I could get this right in the future.\\n2. At the start of prep each day, spend 5 minutes on the Island retrying a question or two.\\n3. If the question clicks, move it to Canoed Away. Um, and, you know, LawHub has a lot of positives in that, you know, this is going to be the actual test-taking platform that you use on the real day of the exam, so of course you want to be really familiar with the interface.\\n- 0:13However, it has a lot of negatives. Especially if you like to Camo. And so I\'m going to outline to you how you can use LawHub in a way that does not hurt your prep here forthwith.\\n- 0:37First thing is, never press complete section. When you finish up a section, you can press complete section and that will then take you to a screen that would show you all of the correct answers\\n- 0:48and all of the answers you chose. And so just review these first two things. Only use self-paste mode. So the other way that they reveal to you your score and impact your ability to Camo is on the selection menu screen where you\'re like picking which PT in which section to do.\\n- 2:22If you like mouse over the section, it then shows you your score. It\'s weird. It feels like it\'s booby trapped, right?\\n- 2:31Like it feels like it\'s booby trapped to not allow you to Camo. And so one way you can avert this, because sometimes people have challenges in that they\'re like, oh, I wanted to go in and open the next section, but I moused over it and I saw the score and then I was upset and blah, blah, blah.\\n- 2:45Is you can just open in different tabs, all of the sections that you plan to do that day or even that week\\n- 2:52and just let them be open tabs. I mean, if I\'m really asking for the world\\n- 7:45and then you are going to apply your backup plan to that answer choice. After you have a full conceptual understanding of the overall meaning of the answer choice.\\n- 7:56I know this is a big ask, but go with me on it. And after you\'ve got that full conceptual understanding of the answer, you aren\'t going to say, oh, this isn\'t relevant or I don\'t like it or it\'s not about boats or whatever.\\n- 8:07You\'re going to actually say, OK, does this make it less likely that Jenna\'s a great runner or does this make it more likely Jenna isn\'t necessarily a great runner\\n- 8:16either way? And it doesn\'t matter to me. How are you going to translate? It\'s fine. But you\'re actually going to ask that backup plan question that\'s informed by the Mission in this case.\\n- 8:24This is the we can enshrine that are really the one time where the backup plan, it\'s really great to put that specificity of the Mission in there because it\'s a goal for the answer to hit.\\n- 8:31And when you ask that question of the answer with all of that nice stimulus specific information in your head, really consciously, really directly, it\'s so much harder to make a mistake because this is literally what the question is asking.\\n- 8:45It\'s just filling in all of those kind of auto complete gaps with like really specific information. So you can\'t accidentally auto complete the wrong way.\\n- 8:53And so I hope that this was helpful. I know I\'m hassling a little bit. It\'s a little bit of eat your vegetables, but I really, really want you to put weak in questions into a better place.\\n- 9:06And so I hope this helped if it didn\'t tell me if it did tell me. I love you.\\n- 9:11I want I want to help you. All right. See you soon.\\n\\n##### Day 10\\n\\n### Take Notes\\n\\n### The Expanded Universe\\n\\n## Video Course Review\\n\\n[\\n\\nConcept Review\\n\\n](https://new.theloophole.com/training-camp/concept-review)\\r\\nFILE: What Is a Necessary Assumption • LO.md\\r\\n---\\ntitle: \\"What Is a Necessary Assumption • LO\\"\\nsource: \\"https://new.theloophole.com/training-camp/browse/stage/learn/video/what-is-a-necessary-assumption?source=timeline&plan=foundation-plan\\"\\nauthor:\\npublished:\\ncreated: 2026-08-30\\ndescription: \\"LSAT prep from Ellen Cassidy, author of The Loophole in LSAT Logical Reasoning — video lessons, drills, and guided study plans.\\"\\ntags:\\n  - \\"clippings\\"\\n---\\n### Ellen\'s Notes\\n\\n**Example**: Dr Shaah\'s research claims that UFOs are real.New evidence of alien bones has recently come to light and carbon testing shows the bones to be of unknown origin. No remains of earthly origin indicate an unknown origin in carbon testing.\\n\\n***Example Necessary Assumptions***\\n\\n1. The presence of alien bones on earth indicates the possibility of UFOs.\\n2. The bones that were recently found qualify as remains.\\n3. Remains of non-earthy origin were not transported to earth by natural cosmological processes.\\n\\n***Ways to Think About a Necessary Assumption (Pick your fav!)***\\n\\n1. If the conclusion is true, the necessary assumption has to be true.\\n2. The author has to believe the necessary assumption.\\n3. The author has to think the necessary assumption is true.\\n4. If the conclusion is proven, the necessary assumption has to be true.\\n5. If the premises worked to prove the conclusion, the necessary assumption is required.\\n6. If these premises proved this conclusion, the author must assume the necessary assumption.\\n7. The argument relies on the necessary assumption.\\n8. For the argument to make any sense, the necessary assumption has to be its foundation.\\n\\n### Take Notes\\n\\n### The Expanded Universe\\n\\nPrep Map\\r\\nFILE: But I Have to Go Fast Intention Controls Retention • LO.md\\r\\n---\\ntitle: \\"But I Have to Go Fast Intention Controls Retention • LO\\"\\nsource: \\"https://new.theloophole.com/training-camp/browse/stage/translation/video/but-i-have-to-go-fast-intention-controls-retention?source=timeline&plan=foundation-plan\\"\\nauthor:\\npublished:\\ncreated: 2026-08-27\\ndescription: \\"LSAT prep from Ellen Cassidy, author of The Loophole in LSAT Logical Reasoning — video lessons, drills, and guided study plans.\\"\\ntags:\\n  - \\"clippings\\"\\n---\\n## But I Have To Go Fast: Intention Controls Retention\\n\\n0:18\\n\\n/\\n\\n8:24\\n\\n- 0:00Everybody loves a rhyme. Intention controls retention. And this is something I want you to keep in mind as you start marinating in the fact that you feel like you need to go faster through this process, faster through a sentence, faster through a stimulus, is that our intentions as we are going\\n- 0:17through a stimulus, a question, a passage, an answer choice, whatever it might be, our intention controls our retention. If our intention is only to be fast, well, the brain will cooperate, right?\\n- 0:29The brain will start just reading topic sentences, scanning for relevant parts, eliminating subvocalization, knowing only that it\'s about coins, things like that.\\n- 0:37It\'s gonna skip over anything that seems like a speed bump, even if that thing is actually quite necessary to knowing what happened in the text that you\'re reading.\\n- 0:46And this sort of process we call ghost reading, right? It\'s what happens when you\'re reading only to be done rather than reading to actually retain information.\\n- 0:55But what I want you to think about right now is a replacement metric of what our intention could be that isn\'t just going faster.\\n- 1:03A pitch that I will make to you is that we want to replace the obsession with going faster and just to be done with going faster toward insight. We want to replace speed to done with a speed to insight metric.\\n- 1:18And so what is insight? Insight is having a kind of epiphany moment of putting information together in a way that analyzes what is going on, concretizes it in some way, works with it in a way that will be helpful to you as you build the world of the stimulus analytically and you move yourself\\n- 1:37toward the kinds of aha, eureka moments that are going to be intrinsic to choosing the correct answer. And that is what the RTQ process is designed to do.\\n- 1:45It\'s designed to optimize mean time from text to insight. And so as you go through your RTQ process, you\'re going to have to get used to optimizing for insight as you go,\\n- 1:57right? And well, that\'s what we all want out of this process anyway. We want it to help us get the right answer.\\n- 2:02So it only makes sense to try and get the right answer, meaning have insights regarding the text that you\'re reading as quickly as possible.\\n- 2:12And of course, in the beginning, like we\'ve talked about in our clipping videos, it can be a little bit difficult to have those insights as you\'re going through text.\\n- 2:21It can be difficult to just translate quickly at all. And you might be asking, okay, but how do I get faster?\\n- 2:29All this stuff about insights, it sounds great, Ellen, to be running toward the correct answer as I\'m in the stimulus, but how do I actually do that fast? Because\\n- 2:36for me, I\'m just there, I read a text, and then, I mean, maybe I can translate it, but that whole like quipping insight moment, I\'m just like, I don\'t know what to say.\\n- 2:45And so the first thing I would tell you is, don\'t skip it. Actually say something. But I would pose an analogous question, which is why do some people run faster than others? Right?\\n- 2:58It\'s because they\'re in better shape. And how do you get in better shape? You run more. I mean, that\'s pretty much the only way.\\n- 3:05Maybe you\'re doing the stair master or an elliptical, or you\'re at the gym doing some other kind of fitness activity. Maybe you\'re a swimmer, who knows? But you\'re doing some kind of fitness activity.\\n- 3:15When people do an RTQ drill with only the purpose of being done and essentially like pantomiming through the drill, but not really actually translating like to the fullest extent of the law, actually quipping to the fullest extent of the law. What they\'re doing is essentially driving to the end\\n- 3:32of the race and hopping over the finish line and saying, look, I did it, I\'m done. And that\'s cool and everything, but I get that you want to be done. And don\'t we all maybe now, I don\'t really wanna be done. I\'m sorry, I wanna empathize with you right now, but I really don\'t wanna\\n- 3:49be done. So that\'s fine to feel, but it\'s not really going to make you better in terms of coming to right answer your eureka moments faster, easier, more intuitively, more effortlessly,\\n- 4:01because here\'s the dirty secret about speed. The worst you are, the more obsessive you are about getting faster. That the more of a conscious thought it is, if I need to go fast,\\n- 4:13I need to go fast, I need to go fast. And I\'m really sorry to say this, but when you\'re a toddler, you need to toddle.\\n- 4:20You need to let yourself have that moment of, oh, I don\'t know what to say next in this Translation. I don\'t know what to say next in this quip.\\n- 4:26And you need to force your brain to build those neural connections, to actually come up with the quip instead of just moving on because you don\'t have time.\\n- 4:35No, this is the part of your prep where you do have time and you\'ve got to force your brain to do it.\\n- 4:41And this is just like, why do some people run faster? Because when they were on their last mile and it was hot and they had a blister, you know what?\\n- 4:48They continued to run. And experts who, you know, they\'re fast and natural at this, they don\'t actually care that much about going fast.\\n- 4:58And you know why? Because they can finish in time naturally. They just go at the pace that they go and everything is fine.\\n- 5:03And so they don\'t actually think that much about the time. I mean, I have later videos about how you know, thinking about time as a way to go slower.\\n- 5:10So like, don\'t allow that talking by your conscious process. But I also, I just want to reiterate one thing for you, which is if you\'re obsessed with this kind of mean time to done metric, then you\'re optimizing yourself to do as little as possible, as quickly as possible, which no wonder we don\'t retain\\n- 5:26much of what we read, right? Engaging in this quest to push you faster and faster in the vain hope you\'re going to get more for less.\\n- 5:34You won\'t. Like we can only get so good at not reading while we\'re reading, you know, there\'s kind of a cap there.\\n- 5:43And so I hope that this convinces you that as you\'re doing these RTQ drills, that you want to optimize for your ability to have takeaways during the stimulus that lead you toward that quest for the correct answer.\\n- 5:57And I understand we\'re doing this all out of order. You know, you don\'t know what the correct answer is, obviously, when you\'re reading the first sentence of the stimulus, but I will tell you that the quiping and Translation process is optimized to lead you on a quicker mean time to insight metric.\\n- 6:13And when you are reading those words, that is the moment where you need to have that quip, have that takeaway, have that engagement with the text, because wisdom is really ready to die on the vine.\\n- 6:25You are going to have the easiest time getting wisdom, insights, actionable takeaways out of what you\'ve read when you have first read that thing.\\n- 6:32Like I recently skimmed, I skimmed, this book, it wasn\'t that great, to be honest.\\n- 6:39I skimmed this book called, some business book, I forget what it\'s called actually, but it\'s the author, the thing that really stood out to me was he stopped the\\n- 6:49chapter and just literally begged people to stop what they were doing right now and open a bank account. And he was so insistent because he was like, you\'re going to get so much more out of this book\\n- 6:59if you actually stop what you are doing right now and do not read anymore, go and do it. And then you go to the next sentence, he\'d be like, did you do it?\\n- 7:08Did you do it? He knows that that action step, and this is what I\'m likening to that quip stage of the RTQ process, the takeaway stage, the actionable insight stage, that that part is so essential that without that, the book just isn\'t going to be that effective.\\n- 7:25And so when you are tempted as you\'re going through your drills to just move on and be like, oh, this is annoying,\\n- 7:33I don\'t really care or want to do this, whatever, I want you to think about what in that moment are you optimizing for?\\n- 7:40What is your intention? Is your intention just to be done, but you don\'t really care about your LSAT score? Or is your intention to actually uplift these skills so that you can get a better LSAT score, more questions right, eventually quicker, but you got to toddle while you toddle.\\n- 7:55And so if you\'re like, listen, girl, I just want to be done and I\'m cool. All right, cool, I respect you.\\n- 8:01Everybody can have their own journey here. But if your goal is to really get questions right, faster, easier, and you want it to be that intuitive analytical process that I\'m talking about, give yourself the grace to breathe while you\'re doing this and don\'t give up just because things don\'t come easy\\n- 8:18in the moment. I believe in you, I know you will grow and change. You just got to be a little bit stubborn with yourself.\\n- 8:23All right, I\'ll see you soon.\\n\\n##### Day 2\\n\\n### Take Notes\\n\\n### The Expanded Universe\\n\\n## Video Course Review\\n\\nConcept Review\\r\\n"}'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.9 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"5e9b97","wall_time_seconds":0.2725589,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":7283,"output":"Warning: truncated output (original token count: 7283)\\nTotal output lines: 916\\n\\nFILE: Accuracy Failsafe • LO.md\\r\\n---\\ntitle: \\"Accuracy Failsafe • LO\\"\\nsource: \\"https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/workouts/accuracy-failsafe\\"\\nauthor:\\npublished:\\ncreated: 2026-08-30\\ndescription: \\"LSAT prep from Ellen Cassidy, author of The Loophole in LSAT Logical Reasoning — video lessons, drills, and guided study plans.\\"\\ntags:\\n  - \\"clippings\\"\\n---\\n## Quick Facts\\n\\n### Will This Help Me?\\n\\nThis will help you if\\n\\nYou need a systematic way to improve your accuracy that will automatically assign you workouts to improve your habits when needed.\\n\\nSkip for now if\\n\\nYou\'re nowhere near your accuracy goals.\\n\\nchallenge indexBuckle Up\\n\\nlevelIntermediate\\n\\nminimum time90+ minutes\\n\\n![clock](https://new.theloophole.com/_next/static/media/clock.f4c45ff9.svg)\\n\\ntiming compatible with35m, 53m, 70m, Marathon, Stopwatch, Natural Pace\\n\\n## Instructions\\n\\n**NOTE**: You can do an Accuracy Failsafe with any workout. Failsafes are time-training protocols that you can apply to any workout.\\n\\n1. Set your accuracy goal as a target number of wrong answers per section.\\n\\t1. An accuracy goal could be -5, for example.\\n2. Complete an LR or RC section\\\\* at your natural pace.\\n3. Take a five-minute break and Camo the section you just completed.\\n\\t1. If you have met your accuracy goal, proceed to a second section of the same type.\\n\\t\\t1. This means if you just completed an RC section and hit your accuracy goal of -5, you would proceed to do another RC section with an accuracy goal of -5.\\n\\t\\t2. If you have not met your accuracy goal on the first section, you have not beaten today\'s Failsafe.\\n\\t\\t1. Do not decrease your target number of wrong answers tomorrow. Keep the same accuracy goal.\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t2. Do not proceed to a second section of the same type today.\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t3. Instead, Wrong Answer Journal and do one Recovery Workout (defined below) now.\\n4. If you have proceeded to a second section, take a five-minute break and Camo it.\\n5. If you have met your accuracy goal for the second time in a row, you have beaten today\'s Failsafe!\\n\\t1. Wrong Answer Journal both of today\'s sections.\\n\\t\\t2. Decrease your target number of wrong answers tomorrow.\\n\\t\\t1. This means that if you beat today\'s Failsafe of -5 accuracy, try for -4 accuracy on tomorrow\'s Failsafe.\\n6. If you do not meet the accuracy goal on the second section, you have not beaten today\'s Failsafe.\\n\\t1. Wrong Answer Journal both of today\'s sections.\\n\\t\\t2. Do not decrease your target number of wrong answers tomorrow. Keep the same accuracy goal.\\n7. Return to Step 1 the following day.\\n\\nSections to use\\n\\nNew Sections Only\\n\\nhow to time\\n\\nCount-Up Stopwatch\\n\\nCountdown Timer\\n\\n## Ellen\'s Advice\\n\\n### If You Want to Win\\n\\n- Failsafes are a great Workout for the student who loves data and structured goals.\\n- Don\'t be intimidated by all the steps you see in the Failsafe.\\n\\t- Once you get into it, the flow of the Workout is relatively intuitive.\\n\\t\\t- Essentially, if you meet your goals twice in a row, you move your goal. If you don\'t meet your goals twice in a row, you don\'t move your goal.\\n- Which Recovery Workout should you choose?\\n\\t- It depends on where you tend to feel difficulty throughout the section.\\n\\t\\t- If you notice you miss questions from not translating the answer choices, going toward an Answer Choice Primer or MemAC could be a great move.\\n\\t\\t- If you notice that you are making strategic mistakes, making a Highlight Reel is a safe bet.\\n\\t\\t- You can be the judge of which Recovery Workout is best for you, but the Recovery Workouts are designed to not allow you to simply repeat the exact same mistakes that you made in the first section of the FailSafe.\\n\\t\\t- Even if you\'re tempted to go outside the list of Recovery Workout, don\'t do one that involves timed question answering.\\n\\n## Real Examples\\n\\n***Recovery Workouts***\\n\\n## The Expanded Universe\\n\\n### Videos to Supercharge Your Workout\\n\\n### Get Help in These Training Blocks\\n\\n1\\r\\nFILE: Camo Review • LO.md\\r\\n---\\ntitle: \\"Camo Review • LO\\"\\nsource: \\"https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/workouts/camo-review\\"\\nauthor:\\npublished:\\ncreated: 2026-08-30\\ndescription: \\"LSAT prep from Ellen Cassidy, author of The Loophole in LSAT Logical Reasoning — video lessons, drills, and guided study plans.\\"\\ntags:\\n  - \\"clippings\\"\\n---\\n## Quick Facts\\n\\n### Will This Help Me?\\n\\nThis will help you if\\n\\nYou are answering questions in any section and need to learn from your mistakes. (This is all of you 🕵🏻)\\n\\nSkip for now if\\n\\nYou have not beaten the Translation + CLIR time and accuracy goals.\\n\\nchallenge indexPretty Chill\\n\\nlevelBeginner\\n\\nminimum time15+ minutes\\n\\n![clock](https://new.theloophole.com/_next/static/media/clock.f4c45ff9.svg)\\n\\ntiming compatible withNot Timed\\n\\n## Instructions\\n\\n1. When you finish an LR or RC section in LawHub, do not press Complete Section. Instead, write down your answers.\\n\\t1. Use Self-Paced Mode on all LR and RC sections. Never use Exam Mode.\\n2. At least 24 hours later, enter your answers into Camo Review.\\n\\t1. **Do not check your answers against the answer key before entering your answers into Camo Review.**\\n3. Camo Review will give you a list of questions to return to.\\n\\t1. This list includes all of your wrong answers and an unknown number of decoy correct answers.\\n4. Reattempt all of the questions in your Camo Review from scratch. Attempt to do a better job on the question now than you did the first time.\\n\\t1. Doing a better job means you should retranslate, requip, and re-CLIR the stimulus on LR or retranslate and requip the passage on RC. Retranslate every part of the question, consider every answer choice, and try to correct your process mistakes before choosing a new answer.\\n5. Enter all your new answers into Camo Review.\\n6. Camo Review will identify all your Misreads, Conceptual Gaps, Self-Doubt, and Self-Confidence answers.\\n7. Your Misreads, Conceptual Gaps, and Self-Doubt answers have been automatically added to your Wrong Answer Journal. Go to your Wrong Answer Journal now to immediately begin the Wrong Answer Journal process.\\n\\nvideo walkthrough\\n\\n[How to Camo](https://new.theloophole.com/training-camp/browse/stage/accuracy/video/how-to-camo)\\n\\nSections to use\\n\\nPreviously Seen Sections Only\\n\\nhow to time\\n\\nUntimed\\n\\n## Ellen\'s Advice\\n\\n### If You Want to Win\\n\\n- Never Camo before doing your sections for the day. Always Camo as the last LSAT thing you do for the day.\\n\\t- If you Camo immediately before completing another section, you will be overly reactive to the result.\\n\\t\\t- If the previous day\'s section went well, this can make you lazy.\\n\\t\\t- If it went poorly, this can make you discouraged.\\n\\t\\t- It\'s not worth taking the risk of either of these reactions affecting your performance on the next section.\\n- The only point of Camo Review is to get a -0.\\n\\t- Do not phone in the Camo Review or rechoose the answer you originally chose out of loyalty because you aren\'t sure it\'s wrong.\\n\\t\\t- If you got more than two questions back in the Camo Review, some of the questions are definitely wrong, so you must change at least some of them.\\n- Do not Camo a section immediately after completing it, unless you are directed explicitly to do so as part of another Workout or Routine.\\n\\t- If you Camo a section immediately after completing it, the Camo score will be worse and you will lose a valuable opportunity to improve.\\n\\n## The Expanded Universe\\n\\n### Videos to Supercharge Your Workout\\n\\n### Similar Workouts You\'ll Probably Enjoy\\n\\nQuick Workout\\n\\n### [Wrong Answer Journal](https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/workouts/wrong-answer-journal)\\n\\nTurn your wrong answers into your goal score\\n\\nLR\\n\\nRC\\n\\n### Turn This Workout Into a Full Routine\\n\\nQuick Routine, 4 Workouts\\n\\n### [PT Off Day Routine](https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/routines/pt-off-day-routine)\\n\\nYour PTs are only as strong as your review\\n\\n🌿 Spaced Set\\n\\nLR\\n\\nRC\\n\\nQuick Routine, 8 Workouts\\n\\n### [Non-Native Speaker Perform Routine](https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/routines/non-native-speaker-perform-routine)\\n\\nYou\'re so hardcore\\n\\n🧱 Daily Foundation\\n\\nLR\\n\\nRC\\n\\nQuick Routine, 3 Workouts\\n\\n### [The Prison Routine](https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/routines/the-prison-routine)\\n\\nDon\'t come out until you can get it right without the stimulus or passage\\n\\n🧱 Daily Foundation\\n\\nLR\\n\\nRC\\n\\nQuick Routine, 5 Workouts\\n\\n### [Welcome to Accuracy Routine (But Really, I Don\'t Want to Party Trick)](https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/routines/welcome-to-accuracy-routine-but-really-i-dont-want-to-party-trick)\\n\\nImprove your accuracy without the scary stuff\\n\\n🧱 Daily keeping your accuracy stable\\n\\n🌿 Spaced Set\\n\\nLR\\n\\nRC\\n\\nRoutine, 8 Workouts\\n\\n### [Non-Native Speaker Accuracy \\"https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/workouts/decaf-rc-translation-drill\\"\\nauthor:\\npublished:\\ncreated: 2026-08-27\\ndescription: \\"LSAT prep from Ellen Cassidy, author of The Loophole in LSAT Logical Reasoning — video lessons, drills, and guided study plans.\\"\\ntags:\\n  - \\"clippings\\"\\n---\\n## Quick Facts\\n\\n### Will This Help Me?\\n\\nThis will help you if\\n\\nYou need to translate an RC passage. (Which is all of you? 😂)\\n\\nSkip for now if\\n\\nYou cannot RTQ a stimulus reliably with good process.\\n\\nchallenge indexBuckle Up\\n\\nlevelBeginner\\n\\nminimum section time32+ minutes\\n\\n![clock](https://new.theloophole.com/_next/static/media/clock.f4c45ff9.svg)\\n\\ntiming compatible withStopwatch\\n\\n## Instructions\\n\\n**NOTE**: Do your recaps out loud in the beginning to keep yourself honest about the quality and completeness of the recaps. Transition to silent recaps when you feel like you\'re ready.\\n\\n1. Read, translate, and quip paragraph 1\\\\*. Do it just like LR.\\n\\t1. This means all the details are included just like they would be on LR.\\n\\t\\t2. Quip after *every* Translation.\\n\\t\\t3. This standard applies to every paragraph you ever translate on RC.\\n2. Look away from paragraph 1 and recap the **FULL TRANSLATION** of paragraph 1 silently.\\n\\t1. The recap is not a summary, gist, or main point.\\n\\t\\t2. The recap should not omit names, dates, and lists.\\n\\t\\t3. The recap should not be a shopping list of information. It should be a story in your own words.\\n\\t\\t4. It is encouraged to mix up the order of the sentences and ideas.\\n\\t\\t5. This standard applies to every paragraph (or collection of paragraphs) you ever recap on RC.\\n3. Read, translate, and quip paragraph 2. Do it just like LR.\\n4. Look away from paragraph 2 and recap the **FULL TRANSLATION** of paragraph 2 silently.\\n5. Without looking back, recap the Translations of paragraphs 1 + 2 back to back silently.\\n6. Read, translate, and quip paragraph 3. Do it just like LR.\\n7. Look away from paragraph 3 and recap the **FULL TRANSLATION** of paragraph 3 silently.\\n8. Without looking back, recap the Translations of paragraphs 1 + 2 + 3 back to back silently.\\n9. Repeat this pattern for all remaining paragraphs.\\n10. Complete this same process on all remaining passages in the section.\\n\\nvideo walkthrough\\n\\n[How and Why to Do Decaf RC Translation](https://new.theloophole.com/training-camp/browse/stage/translation/video/how-and-why-to-do-decaf-rc-translation)\\n\\nSections to use\\n\\nMix in Previously Seen Sections\\n\\nhow to time\\n\\nCount-Up Stopwatch\\n\\nper passage time goal to move on\\n\\n12 min (outloud recaps), 8 min (silent recaps)\\n\\n## Ellen\'s Advice\\n\\n### If You Want to Win\\n\\n- If you have a 70-minute time accommodation, you can use Decaf RC Translation on test day. Complete six to eight Translation + CLIR Drills\\\\* in one sitting with no breaks.\\n\\nSections to use\\n\\nNew Sections Only\\n\\nhow to time\\n\\nCount-Up Stopwatch\\n\\n## Ellen\'s Advice\\n\\n### If You Want to Win\\n\\n- **It is very important that you do not take breaks between sections or check your sections in the middle of the Marathon.**\\n\\t- Marathons only work because your brain doesn’t get the chance to re-adapt to non-LSAT thinking.\\n\\t\\t- Taking breaks guarantees that the Marathon will not work.\\n\\t\\t- Do not check your phone.\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t- Do not have conversations.\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t- Do not take a lunch break.\\n\\t\\t- Put snacks and drinks on the desk to eat while you Marathon, if needed.\\n\\t\\t- The only breaks allowed during Translation Marathons are medically required or biologically required breaks.\\n\\t\\t- Running to the bathroom is allowed between sections.\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t- Accommodated breaks for medically required tasks (like insulin injections, etc) are obviously allowed.\\n- Open all the sections you plan to complete in separate tabs before starting the Translation Marathon.\\n- Keep track of the order of the sections in your Translation Marathon, so you can analyze the results.\\n- If you feel yourself getting worse as the Translation Marathon progresses, you are likely becoming fatigued.\\n\\t- This is not cause for alarm. Fatigue is part of the process; it indicates that you need to build stamina.\\n- At the end of the Translation Marathon, take a break and then check all the sections against the CLIR Answer Key in a big batch.\\n\\n### Timing Tips\\n\\n- Set a section timer for 400 minutes in LawHub\'s Self-Paced Mode. **Do not use Exam Mode.**\\n- Time each Translation + CLIR Drill with an external stopwatch. Record the time each drill takes in your tracker.\\n\\n## The Expanded Universe\\n\\n### Videos to Supercharge Your Workout\\n\\n### Get Help in These Training Blocks\\n\\n![How to Translate in 20 Minutes](https://new.theloophole.com/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fvz-3500a044-c45.b-cdn.net%2Fbcdn_token%3DHS256-pRt1zt2ihN3B46jcEW5gdBaM5t-ai0GnFLSvCSEvoZ0%26token_path%3D%252F6dbdf370-9b76-425c-9547-58127fbea4e7%252F%26expires%3D1788739200%2F6dbdf370-9b76-425c-9547-58127fbea4e7%2Fthumbnail_8a0fe291.jpg&w=3840&q=75)\\n\\n### How to Translate in 20 Minutes\\n\\n25 of 25 Videos Watched\\n\\n25 videos22 min\\n\\n### Similar Workouts You\'ll Probably Enjoy\\n\\nWorkout\\n\\n### [Translation + CLIR Drill](https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/workouts/translation-plus-clir-drill)\\n\\nTransform your reading for LSAT mastery\\n\\nMost Popular\\n\\nMost Repeated\\n\\nMost Improved\\n\\nLR\\n\\n### Turn This Workout Into a Full Routine\\n\\nRoutine, 4 Workouts\\n\\n### [Translation Foundation Routine](https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/routines/translation-foundation-routine)\\n\\nThe most important Routine in Loophole Online\\n\\n🧱 Daily Foundation\\n\\nCommunity Favorite\\n\\nTop Performing\\n\\nLR\\n\\nRC\\n\\nRoutine, 5 Workouts\\n\\n### [Translation + Decaf Routine](https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/routines/translation-plus-decaf-routine)\\n\\nThis might be the hardest Routine you do in Loophole Online\\n\\n🧱 Daily Foundation\\n\\nCommunity Favorite\\n\\nMost Repeated\\n\\nLR\\n\\nRC\\n\\n### Helps With These Prep Map Points### Foundation Translation Routine\\n\\nTranslation - \\"clippings\\"\\n---\\n## Quick Facts\\n\\n### Will This Help Me?\\n\\nThis will help you if\\n\\nYou take longer than 5 seconds to diagram Unless or you make mistakes diagramming Unless greater than 1% of the time\\n\\nSkip for now if\\n\\nYou diagram Unless automatically in less than 5 seconds 100% of the time\\n\\nchallenge indexPretty Chill\\n\\nlevelBeginner\\n\\nminimum time3+ minutes\\n\\n![clock](https://new.theloophole.com/_next/static/media/clock.f4c45ff9.svg)\\n\\ntiming compatible withStopwatch\\n\\n## Instructions\\n\\n1. Diagram three out of these four Unless statements:\\n\\t1. ~A, unless B.\\n\\t\\t2. Unless B, A.\\n\\t\\t3. Unless ~B, ~A.\\n\\t\\t4. A, unless ~B.\\n2. Switch up which three statements you diagram every time.\\n3. Start every LSAT study session with this quick workout until you can diagram Unless accurately in under 5 seconds.\\n\\nSections to use\\n\\nDoesn\'t Use a Section\\n\\nhow to time\\n\\nCount-Up Stopwatch\\n\\ntime goal to move on\\n\\n15 sec\\n\\n## Ellen\'s Advice\\n\\n### If You Want to Win\\n\\n- Don\'t simply memorize the correct diagrams. Diagram each statement fresh every time.\\n- Change the letters in the statements.\\n- You\'re working too hard to lose points on your real LSAT because you didn\'t really get Unless.\\n\\n### Timing Tips\\n\\n- It shouldn\'t take you more than five seconds to diagram an individual Unless statement once you really understand how Unless works.\\n- The entire Routine shouldn\'t take you more than 15 seconds total once you\'re trained\\n\\t- I just diagrammed three Unless statements in 9 seconds to prove it can be done.\\n- If the Unless Practice Routine takes you much longer than 15 seconds, it just shows how much you need to do it\\n- Once you hit the point where you can diagram three Unless statements in 15 seconds, you can lower your Unless Practice Routine frequency to once per week.\\n\\n## Real Examples\\n\\n***Unless Practice Routine Answer Key***\\r\\nFILE: Wrong Answer Journal • LO.md\\r\\n---\\ntitle: \\"Wrong Answer Journal • LO\\"\\nsource: \\"https://new.theloophole.com/workouts-and-routines/workouts/wrong-answer-journal\\"\\nauthor:\\npublished:\\ncreated: 2026-08-30\\ndescription: \\"LSAT prep from Ellen Cassidy, author of The Loophole in LSAT Logical Reasoning — video lessons, drills, and guided study plans.\\"\\ntags:\\n  - \\"clippings\\"\\n---\\n## Quick Facts\\n\\n### Will This Help Me?\\n\\nThis will help you if\\n\\nYou need to improve your accuracy and stop making the same mistakes over and over again.\\n\\nSkip for now if\\n\\nWrong Answer Journals are harmless.\\n\\nchallenge indexPretty Chill\\n\\nlevelBeginner\\n\\nminimum time15+ minutes\\n\\n![clock](https://new.theloophole.com/_next/static/media/clock.f4c45ff9.svg)\\n\\ntiming compatible withNot Timed\\n\\n## Instructions\\n\\n1. After you\'ve Camoed your LR or RC section, begin the Wrong Answer Journaling process.\\n2. For each wrong answer, fill in the date you did the section, the question number that was wrong, and the answer you chose.\\n\\t1. This information will already be filled in for you if you Camo using Loophole Online.\\n3. Fill in your Reason Missed column\\n\\t1. In this column, write down **what you did wrong that caused you to miss this question**.\\n\\t\\t1. Ask yourself, \\"Why did I think that this wrong answer was right? Why did I think that this right answer was wrong?\\" Focus on changing the actions that enabled these mistaken judgments.\\n\\t\\t2. The first word of every entry in this column should be \\"I.\\"\\n\\t\\t1. Example: \\"I misidentified the question stem.\\"\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t2. This will force you to reflect on **what you did** that caused you to choose the wrong answer and overlook the right answer.\\n4. Fill in your Solution column\\n\\t1. In this column, write down **what you will do differently to ensure you don\'t make this same mistake again**.\\n\\t\\t1. Ask yourself, \\"What could I have done that would have made me realize in the moment that the wrong answer is wrong? What could I have done that would have made me realize in the moment that the right answer is right?\\"\\n\\t\\t2. The first words of every entry in this column should be \\"I will...\\"\\n\\t\\t1. Example: \\"I will do Question Stem Training to improve my ability to correctly identify question types.\\"\\n\\t\\t3. Frame your Solution Column as a direct response to the Reason Missed column.\\n\\nClick [here](https://new.theloophole.com/review-and-camo/wrong-answer-journal) to go to your Wrong Answer Journal\\n\\nvideo walkthrough\\n\\n[How to Use the Wrong Answer Journal](https://new.theloophole.com/training-camp/browse/stage/accuracy/video/how-to-use-the-wrong-answer-journal)\\n\\nSections to use\\n\\nPreviously Seen Sections Only\\n\\nhow to time\\n\\nUntimed\\n\\n## Ellen\'s Advice\\n\\n### If You Want to Win\\n\\n- Keep your Wrong Answer Journal entries short and sweet. One to two sentences is enough for each column.\\n- **Do not waste time in your Wrong Answer Journal writing down detailed explanations of why the answer you chose was wrong, and why the right answer is right in the exact context of the question.**\\n\\t- If you\'re writing anything that cannot be ported directly to subsequent sections, you are wasting your Wrong Answer Journal time.\\n- The Wrong Answer Journal gives you a chance to identify the exact **actions you took** that made you make the wrong decision. It then gives you the opportunity to decide which actions you will take in the future that will enable you to make correct decisions. This is the essence of improvement on the LSAT.\\n\\t- I want you to know why an answer is right and another is wrong, but I don\'t want you to stop there. I want you to start there.\\n\\t\\t- After you know why the correct answer is correct and why the wrong answer is wrong, look more deeply at your actions and mindset in relation to the question.\\n\\t\\t- What did you do that caused this result? And what will you do to ensure a different result next time?\\n- Do not rely on cheap explanations in your Reason Missed column like \\"silly mistake,\\" \\"misread,\\" or \\"rushing.\\"\\n\\t- Dive into why you made this silly mistake, why you misread, and why you were rushing.\\n\\t\\t- This is how you can identify the problematic actions that lead to problematic judgments and change them for the better.\\n\\n## The Expanded Universe\\n\\n### Videos to Supercharge Your Workout\\n\\n### Get Help in These Training Blocks\\n\\n1\\n\\n### Similar Workouts You\'ll Probably Enjoy\\n\\nQuick Pop Quiz: Assumption vs. Objection\\n\\n## Part 1 — Trigger Phrases\\n\\nFor each trigger phrase, identify whether it signals an **Assumption** or an **Objection**, then state what to do with the variable.\\n\\n| # | Trigger Phrase | Identify (Circle) | What Do We Do? |\\n|---|---|---|---|\\n| 1 | Takes for granted that X | Assumption / Objection | __________ |\\n| 2 | Fails to consider that Y | Assumption / Objection | __________ |\\n| 3 | Presumes that X | Assumption / Objection | __________ |\\n| 4 | Overlooks the possibility that Y | Assumption / Objection | __________ |\\n| 5 | Ignores the possibility that Y | Assumption / Objection | __________ |\\n| 6 | Assumes that X | Assumption / Objection | __________ |\\n| 7 | Fails to establish that X | Assumption / Objection | __________ |\\n| 8 | Neglects to consider that Y | Assumption / Objection | __________ |\\n\\n## Part 2 — Real Answer Choices\\n\\n| # | Answer Choice | Identify | What Do We Do? |\\n|---|---|---|---|\\n| 9 | \\"The author fails to consider that there are probably species in existence that have not yet been identified.\\" | *[Example]* Objection | *If it is **TRUE** that there are probably species not yet identified, would that weaken the conclusion?* |\\n| 10 | \\"Ignores the possibility that the bottle contained an acidic liquid other than vinegar.\\" | Assumption / Objection | __________ |\\n| 11 | \\"Assumes that the fact of a labeling error is proof of an intention to deceive.\\" | Assumption / Objection | __________ |\\n| 12 | \\"Ignores the possibility that some food additives are harmful to most people.\\" | Assumption / Objection | __________ |\\n| 13 | \\"Presumes that most consumers heed the warning labels on beverage containers.\\" | Assumption / Objection | __________ |\\n| 14 | \\"Fails to consider that the economy might not improve.\\" | Assumption / Objection | __________ |\\n| 15 | \\"Ignores the possibility that the city council is not the only body that can make such decisions.\\" | Assumption / Objection | __________ |\\n| 16 | \\"Assumes that the economy will improve in the near future.\\" | Assumption / Objection | __________ |\\n\\n---\\n\\n# Full Answer Key\\n\\n## Part 1: Generic Drill Key\\n\\n| # | Trigger Phrase | Type | What Do We Do? (The Correct Test) |\\n|---|---|---|---|\\n| 1 | Takes for granted that X | **Assumption** | Make X **FALSE**. Does the argument crash? |\\n| 2 | Fails to consider that Y | **Objection** | Take Y to be **TRUE**. Does the conclusion die? |\\n| 3 | Presumes that X | **Assumption** | Make X **FALSE**. Does the argument crash? |\\n| 4 | Overlooks the possibility that Y | **Objection** | Take Y to be **TRUE**. Does the conclusion die? |\\n| 5 | Ignores the possibility that Y | **Objection** | Take Y to be **TRUE**. Does the conclusion die? |\\n| 6 | Assumes that X | **Assumption** | Make X **FALSE**. Does the argument crash? |\\n| 7 | Fails to establish that X | **Assumption** | Make X **FALSE**. Does the argument crash? |\\n| 8 | Neglects to consider that Y | **Objection** | Take Y to be **TRUE**. Does the conclusion die? |\\n\\n## Part 2: Real LSAT Answer Key\\n\\n| # | Type | What Do We Do? (The Correct Logic Check) |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| 9 | **Objection** | \\"If it is TRUE that there are probably species not yet identified, would that weaken the conclusion?\\" |\\n| 10 | **Objection** | \\"If it is TRUE that the bottle contained an acidic liquid other than vinegar, would that weaken the conclusion?\\" |\\n| 11 | **Assumption** | \\"If a labeling error is NOT proof of an intention to deceive, does the argument crash?\\" |\\n| 12 | **Objection** | \\"If it is TRUE that some food additives are harmful to most people, would that weaken the conclusion?\\" |\\n| 13 | **Assumption** | \\"If most consumers do NOT heed the warning labels, does the argument crash?\\" |\\n| 14 | **Objection** | \\"If it is TRUE that the economy might not improve, would that weaken the conclusion?\\" |\\n| 15 | **Objection** | \\"If it is TRUE that the city council is not the only body that can make such decisions, would that weaken the conclusion?\\" |\\n| 16 | **Assumption** | \\"If the economy will NOT improve in the near future, does the argument crash?\\" |\\n\\n---\\n\\n*Source: Google Doc `1lVgT7E8QPfOuSfLYrck1AsdsiZZ-Iyk530VfUIxrMF0` (titled \\"Flaw.gdoc\\").
